<!--
Hi everyone! I’m Josh – I’m a full time independent open source maintainer. I focus on static analysis tooling around JavaScript & TypeScript – most notably, typescript-eslint, the tooling that allows you to run ESLint and Prettier on TypeScript code.
I’m also the author of the recent Learning TypeScript book, published by O’Reilly.
-->

<!--
Let’s take a look at the Codecademy learner dashboard page, which showcases a few design system practices. This slide’s background image is a screenshot of codecademy.com/learn for a signed-in user.
For many, “design system” means the low-level “atoms” that form the basic building blocks of the page: colors, buttons, typography, and iconography. That’s a huge component of many design systems and is incredibly important to get right. This page has quite a few examples:
Colors: white, gray, etc.
Text presets: caption, menu, h2, h3, body
Buttons: “TextButton” and “FillButton”
Iconography and logos
More unusual or niche ones: a ProgressBar and a HexCharacter
-->

---

layout: none
transition: none
---
